In the same month, he was awarded the Best Goal of the [2025 Campeonato Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Campeonato_Paulista) for the direct corner kick goal in February.[^cite_note-\:71-402] On 16 April, Neymar wore the number 100 jersey for the match against [Atlético Mineiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clube_Atl%C3%A9tico_Mineiro) which resulted in a 2–0 win, landmarking his 100th match at the [Vila Belmiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Est%C3%A1dio_Urbano_Caldeira); he would feature in only 32 minutes during the match, however, and was subbed off after suffering an injury.[^cite_note-403]

Neymar spent more than a month sidelined before returning in [2025 Copa do Brasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Copa_do_Brasil) match against [CRB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clube_de_Regatas_Brasil) in [Maceió](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macei%C3%B3) on 22 May; coming on as a second-half substitute, he converted his penalty in the shoot-out, but his side was knocked out.[^cite_note-404] Neymar returned to the starting line-up on 1 June in a match against [Botafogo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botafogo_de_Futebol_e_Regatas). Deployed as a [false nine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_nine), he played for 76 minutes in the match before being sent off after [handballing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handball_\(association_football\)); Santos eventually lost the match 1–0, with the opponent's goal coming ten minutes after his ejection.[^cite_note-405]

On 24 June, Santos announced Neymar's contract renewal until the end of the year, with an option for a further six months.[^cite_note-406] The club's president Marcelo Teixeira said that Neymar's return to Santos has led to record-breaking social media growth and significant commercial gains for the club.[^cite_note-407] On his first match after the renewal, he scored the winner in a 1–0 home win over Flamengo on 16 July.[^cite_note-408]

On 4 August, Neymar scored twice in a 3–1 win against [Juventude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esporte_Clube_Juventude), increasing Santos' hopes of remaining in the top division.[^cite_note-409] In September, Santos coach [Juan Pablo Vojvoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Pablo_Vojvoda) praised Neymar as "one of the greatest in history".[^cite_note-410] In the same month, he suffered another [thigh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thigh) injury while training,[^cite_note-411] and spent 48 days sidelined before returning to action.[^cite_note-412] Later that year, on 3 December, he scored his first hat-trick since his return in a 3–0 away win over Juventude, helping Santos to move out the relegation zone.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] The goals took his goal tally for Santos to 150.[^cite_note-415]

#### 2026–

On 6 January 2026, Neymar extended his contract with Santos until the end of the year.[^cite_note-416] On 26 February, he led Santos to a 2–1 victory over [Vasco da Gama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CR_Vasco_da_Gama), scoring both goals.[^cite_note-417] On 26 July, he scored twice against [Chapecoense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associa%C3%A7%C3%A3o_Chapecoense_de_Futebol) in a 2–2 draw, scoring the equaliser from the penalty spot in the final minutes of the game.[^cite_note-418] On 4 August, Neymar assisted the only goal as Santos defeated [Remo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clube_do_Remo) 1–0 in the [2026 Copa do Brasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Copa_do_Brasil) to progress to the quarter-finals, however, after the match tension was ignited between the two teams, with Neymar being captured on video taunting the staff of Remo by shouting "eliminated, eliminated".[^cite_note-\:70-419] On 13 August, Neymar was decisive in the [Copa Sudamericana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Copa_Sudamericana) match against [Macará](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_Deportivo_Macar%C3%A1), assisting both goals in a 2–1 comeback victory.[^cite_note-420]

In September 2026, during a match against rivals [Palmeiras](187243.md) which sealed Santos' elimination from the national cup, Neymar suffered another thigh injury, being subbed off at half-time.[^cite_note-421] He was also at the centre of controversy weeks later; after being caught mocking the [Atlético Mineiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clube_Atl%C3%A9tico_Mineiro) players after a 2–0 home win for the first leg of the Copa Sudamericana quarter-finals, he was himself a target of mockery after the club's elimination on penalties.[^cite_note-422]

## International career

### 2010–2012: Senior debut and first major tournaments



Neymar at a press conference for [Brazil](149286.md) in August 2010. He made his senior debut that month aged 18.

Following Neymar's performances for Santos in their successful early 2010 season, including winning the [2010 Copa do Brasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Copa_do_Brasil), in which he was the top goalscorer, and the [2010 Campeonato Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Campeonato_Paulista), in which he scored 14 goals, former Brazilian football players Pelé and Romário reportedly urged coach [Dunga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunga) to take Neymar to the [2010 World Cup](168079.md).[^cite_note-Brazilians_want_Neymar-423] Although the opinion that Neymar deserved a place in Dunga's squad went as far as a 14,000 signature petition,[^cite_note-Brazilians_want_Neymar-423][^cite_note-424] and despite the pressure on Dunga to pick Neymar,[^cite_note-425] he was omitted from both the squad of 23,[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427] and the stand-by list.[^cite_note-428] Although Dunga described Neymar as "extremely talented", he said that the 18-year-old had not been tested sufficiently on the international level to earn a World Cup spot and he had failed to impress enough while on international duty.[^cite_note-429]

On 26 July 2010, Neymar was selected for the first time in the [Brazil senior team](149286.md) by new head coach [Mano Menezes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mano_Menezes) for a friendly match against the [United States](582488.md) to be played in [East Rutherford, New Jersey](136878.md). On 10 August 2010, he made his debut with the national team in that game, aged 18 years old, starting the match and wearing the number 11 jersey. He scored on his debut after 28 minutes, a header coming from an [André Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Santos) cross in a 2–0 win for Brazil.[^cite_note-430] On 1 March 2011, Neymar said: "Being on the Brazilian team is a privilege. There are some marvelous players and I'm very happy to be among them."[^cite_note-Samba_Star-8]



Neymar [celebrates his goal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goal_celebration) for Brazil against [Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_national_football_team), on 27 March 2011, with [André Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Santos) and [Ramires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramires).

On 27 March 2011, he scored twice in a 2–0 win against [Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_national_football_team) at the [Emirates Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirates_Stadium).[^cite_note-431] After he scored from the penalty spot, a banana was thrown onto the pitch, leading Neymar to complain about 'constant jeering and an atmosphere of racism' from the stands.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433] While Scottish officials explained that Neymar had been booed solely for perceived injury-feigning, a German student who was in the stadium amongst Brazil supporters said that he threw the banana with no racist intentions. This led to the [Scottish Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Football_Association) asking the [Brazilian Football Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Football_Confederation) for an apology for the accusations made to the Scottish fans.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435] Neymar refused to apologise or retract his words, claiming he "did not accuse any person or any group of supporters".[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

Neymar was the leading goalscorer of the [2011 South American Youth Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_South_American_Youth_Championship) with nine goals, including two in the final, in Brazil's 6–0 win against Uruguay.[^cite_note-438] He also took part at the [2011 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica) in [Argentina](18951905.md), where he scored two goals in the first-round game against [Ecuador](1016797.md). He was selected 'Man of the Match' in Brazil's first match against [Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuela_national_football_team), which ended a [1–1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica_Group_B#Brazil_v_Venezuela) draw. Brazil were eliminated in the quarter-finals in a [penalty shoot-out](511302.md) against [Paraguay](804025.md) (2–2 [a.e.t.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overtime_\(association_football\))), with Neymar being substituted in the 80th minute.[^cite_note-439]



Neymar with Brazilian teammate [Lucas Moura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Moura) on international duty in 2012

By 20 years old, Neymar was viewed as the star player of the national team.[^cite_note-440] At this point, he was described as "a national demigod in Brazil" by *[SportsPro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SportsPro)*.[^cite_note-441] On 11 May 2012, Neymar was selected for the squad of the [Brazil Olympic football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil_Olympic_football_team) to participate in the [London 2012 Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_2012_Olympic_Games).[^cite_note-442] In Brazil's first warm-up match on 20 July 2012, against the host nation [Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Britain_national_football_team) at [Riverside Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverside_Stadium), Neymar was involved in both goals of a 2–0 win, first making an assist with a free-kick into the six-yard box for [Sandro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_\(footballer,_born_1989\))'s header, before converting a penalty.[^cite_note-443] On 26 July, he scored his first goal in Brazil's opening fixture against [Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt_national_under-23_football_team), which ended a 3–2 victory for Brazil.[^cite_note-444] In the following match against [Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus_national_under-23_football_team) at [Old Trafford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Trafford) in Manchester, Neymar scored a free-kick from 25-yards into the top-right corner of the goal and set up goals for [Alexandre Pato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_Pato)'s header with a cross from the right and [Oscar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_\(footballer,_born_1991\)) with a back heel as Brazil secured their place in the quarter-finals with a 3–1 win. Afterwards he said: "I scored and made two assists so for me it was perfect".[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446]

On 5 August 2012, in the quarter-final encounter against [Honduras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honduras_national_under-23_football_team), Neymar scored a penalty, his third goal of the tournament and assisted in [Leandro Damião](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leandro_Dami%C3%A3o)'s second goal on the match, to help Brazil to a 3–2 victory at [St James' Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_James'_Park) and book a place in the semi-finals.[^cite_note-447] On 11 August, Brazil lost 2–1 to [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico_national_under-23_football_team) in the final at [Wembley Stadium](8913012.md) in London.[^cite_note-448]

Neymar scored his first full international hat-trick on 10 September 2012, in an 8–0 win over [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_national_football_team) at the [Estádio José do Rego Maciel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Est%C3%A1dio_Jos%C3%A9_do_Rego_Maciel) in [Recife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recife).[^cite_note-449] On 19 September, Neymar scored the winner against Argentina in a 2–1 win in the first leg of the [2012 Superclásico de las Américas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Supercl%C3%A1sico_de_las_Am%C3%A9ricas) at the [Estádio Serra Dourada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Est%C3%A1dio_Serra_Dourada) in [Goiânia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goi%C3%A2nia), Brazil.[^cite_note-450]

### 2013–2014: Confederations Cup triumph and World Cup breakthrough



Neymar playing against Spain's [Gerard Piqué](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerard_Piqu%C3%A9) in the [2013 FIFA Confederations Cup Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_FIFA_Confederations_Cup_Final)

Neymar was selected as part of [Luiz Felipe Scolari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_Felipe_Scolari)'s Brazil squad for the [2013 Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_FIFA_Confederations_Cup) on home soil. For the tournament he was assigned the number 10 shirt, having previously worn 11.[^cite_note-451]

Neymar scored the first goal of the tournament in a 3–0 win over [Japan](993546.md) at the [Estádio Nacional Mané Garrincha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Est%C3%A1dio_Nacional_Man%C3%A9_Garrincha) on 15 June.[^cite_note-452] In their second match, Neymar scored after nine minutes and crossed for [Jô](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B4)'s late goal to give Brazil a 2–0 win over [Mexico](808402.md).[^cite_note-453] He scored in his third consecutive match, with a powerful free kick from the edge of the penalty area, as Brazil beat [Italy](362466.md) 4–2, and received his third consecutive man-of-the-match award.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455] On 30 June, Neymar was involved in [Fred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_\(footballer,_born_1983\))'s opening goal and then subsequently scored Brazil's second in the 3–0 [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_FIFA_Confederations_Cup_Final) victory over [Spain](609825.md).[^cite_note-Brazil_3–0_Spain-456] Neymar's performances saw him receive the [Golden Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Ball_\(FIFA_Confederations_Cup\)) for player of the tournament.[^cite_note-prosoccertalk.nbcsports.com-457] He also finished in 5th place for the [2013 FIFA Ballon d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_FIFA_Ballon_d'Or).[^cite_note-458]

On 5 March 2014, Neymar scored a hat-trick in a friendly win against [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_national_football_team) in [Johannesburg](58893.md). He made headlines for his conduct after the final whistle when a South African boy ran onto the pitch. As security staff began to escort the boy from the field, Neymar intervened and introduced him to his Brazil teammates before they lifted him in the air during their celebrations.[^cite_note-South_Africa-459][^cite_note-460]



Neymar prepares to take a free kick during Brazil's opening match of the [2014 FIFA World Cup](656933.md) against Croatia.



Neymar evading a tackle against [Mexico](808402.md) at the 2014 World Cup

On 2 June, Neymar was named in the Brazil squad for the [2014 World Cup](656933.md).[^cite_note-461] Going into the tournament as the team's star player, Neymar was expected to lead Brazil to their sixth World Cup success, and first title on home soil.[^cite_note-462][^cite_note-A_NATION_HOPES-463][^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466] One week prior to the team's opening match, Neymar scored once and made two assists in a 4–0 friendly win over [Panama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_national_football_team).[^cite_note-467]

Neymar earned his 50th international cap in the opening match of the tournament, on 12 June against [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team) in [São Paulo](390875.md). In the 26th minute, with Croatia leading 1–0, Neymar elbowed Croatian midfielder [Luka Modrić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Modri%C4%87), after which Neymar was issued a [yellow card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_card_\(association_football\)).[^cite_note-Squawka_News_Brazil_Croatia-468] Many critics agreed that Neymar's punishment was too lenient and that he should have been issued a [red card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_card_\(association_football\)).[^cite_note-Japanese_fans_regret_and_26th_minute-469][^cite_note-Independent_Online_Brazil_Croatia-470][^cite_note-Firstpost_Brazil_Croatia-471] He equalised before half-time with a shot from outside the box and gave Brazil the lead in the second half with a penalty kick, following a [controversial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_2014_FIFA_World_Cup_controversies#Brazil_vs_Croatia) by the referee, in an eventual 3–1 win.[^cite_note-472] In the third group match, he again scored twice as the *Seleção* defeated [Cameroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameroon_national_football_team) 4–1 to reach the [knockout stage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage).[^cite_note-473] In the [round of 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage#Round_of_16) against [Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile_national_football_team), the match finished 1–1 after 120 minutes and was decided by a penalty shoot-out with Neymar scoring what proved to be the winning kick for Brazil.[^cite_note-beeb-474]

In the [quarter-final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage#Quarter-finals) win over [Colombia](680175.md), while challenging for the ball, Neymar was kneed in the back by [Juan Camilo Zúñiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Camilo_Z%C3%BA%C3%B1iga) and had to be removed from the pitch on a stretcher. A hospital scan revealed that Neymar had suffered a [fractured vertebra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinal_fracture) in his spine and he missed the rest of the World Cup.[^cite_note-475] Earlier in the match, Neymar assisted Thiago Silva's opening goal with a cross from a corner kick.[^cite_note-476] It was the second Neymar assist from a corner in the tournament, after creating [David Luiz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Luiz)'s goal in the previous round against Chile.[^cite_note-beeb-474] Without their injured talisman Neymar (and the suspended captain Thiago Silva), Brazil would ultimately lose in the semi-final in a stunning [7–1 defeat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil_v_Germany_\(2014_FIFA_World_Cup\)) to [eventual champion Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_Final) on 8 July.[^cite_note-477]

On 11 July, Neymar was named on the 10-man shortlist for the World Cup [Golden Ball](656933.md#Golden_Ball) award for the tournament's best player.[^cite_note-478] He won the [Bronze Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Shoe#Golden_Boot) as the tournament's third top goalscorer and was voted in the [Dream Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Shoe#All-Star_Team).[^cite_note-479]

Having scored 15 goals for Brazil in 2014, Neymar finished as runner-up for the [IFFHS World's Best International Goal Scorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFFHS_World's_Best_International_Goal_Scorer) award, the second time he had done so after [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFFHS_World's_Best_International_Goal_Scorer#List_of_winners).[^cite_note-\:73-480]

### 2015–2016: Assuming the captaincy and Olympic gold



Neymar captaining Brazil during a friendly match against [Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile_national_football_team) on 29 March 2015

With captain Thiago Silva ruled out through injury, new coach [Dunga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunga) decided to make Neymar captain and confirmed on 5 September that the forward would stay on as skipper on a permanent basis.[^cite_note-481] On 14 October 2014, Neymar scored four goals in one game for the first time in his international career, scoring all of Brazil's goals in 4–0 friendly win against Japan at the [National Stadium, Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Stadium,_Singapore).[^cite_note-Japan-482] At the age of just 22 years, Neymar had scored 40 goals in 58 internationals, and had become the [fifth highest goalscorer](149286.md#Top_goalscorers) for his national team.[^cite_note-Japan-482][^cite_note-RSSSF-483] On 26 March 2015, Neymar scored Brazil's second goal in a 3–1 friendly win over [France](250197.md) in Paris.[^cite_note-484]

On 14 June 2015, in Brazil's opening [Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica) fixture, Neymar scored the equaliser and assisted the stoppage-time winning goal by [Douglas Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Costa) as Brazil came from 0–1 down to beat [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peru_national_football_team) 2–1 in [Temuco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temuco).[^cite_note-485] After Brazil's second match, a 0–1 loss to [Colombia](680175.md) in [Santiago](51572.md), Neymar was booked for handball, resulting in a suspension. After the final whistle, he was [red carded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ejection_\(sports\)) for deliberately kicking the ball at [Pablo Armero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Armero), and as a result was pushed over by Colombian striker [Carlos Bacca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Bacca), who was also dismissed.[^cite_note-486] [CONMEBOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONMEBOL) issued Neymar with a four-match ban, ruling him out for the remainder of the tournament, in addition to a \$10000 fine.[^cite_note-487]

The CBF had wanted Neymar to play at the [Copa América Centenario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Am%C3%A9rica_Centenario) and the [Olympics in Rio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_tournament) during the summer of 2016, but after a request from Barcelona manager Luis Enrique, he was rested from the first tournament.[^cite_note-488] In late June 2016, he was subsequently one of the three over-23 players to be included in [Brazil's squad for the 2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_team_squads#Brazil) on home soil, and was named the team's captain by the Olympic side's manager [Rogério Micale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rog%C3%A9rio_Micale).[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490]

In Brazil's quarter-final clash with [Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombia_Olympic_football_team) on 13 August, Neymar scored Brazil's first goal of the match, from a [direct free kick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_free_kick), and also set up his nation's second goal in an eventual 2–0 win.[^cite_note-491] In Brazil's semi-final clash with [Honduras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honduras_national_under-23_football_team) on 18 August, Neymar scored twice, Brazil's first and last goals of the match, in a 6–0 win.[^cite_note-492]



Neymar preparing to strike the winning penalty kick for Brazil against Germany in the [Olympic final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_tournament#Gold_medal_match)

In the final against Germany at the [Maracanã](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maracan%C3%A3_Stadium) in Rio on 20 August, Neymar opened the scoring with a free-kick after 27 minutes. The game finished 1–1 after [Max Meyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Meyer_\(footballer\)) equalised in the second-half. Brazil beat Germany 5–4 on penalties, and Neymar scored the winning penalty to bring Brazil its first Olympic gold medal in men's football.[^cite_note-493][^cite_note-494] He was hailed as a hero in Brazil for helping end the nation's conquest for an Olympic gold medal.[^cite_note-495] Neymar was named by the [Brazilian Olympic Committee](2567594.md) as the best footballer of the Olympics.[^cite_note-\:27-265]

Both during and prior to the tournament, Neymar had been subjected to criticism over his conduct on and off the pitch, with several former Brazil players suggesting that he was not fit to captain the national team.[^cite_note-496] Neymar subsequently renounced the captaincy following their Olympic victory.[^cite_note-497]

On 10 November, Neymar scored his 50th goal for Brazil in a 3–0 win in the [2018 World Cup qualifier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification) against Argentina, doing so at 24 years old; this made him the third-youngest player to reach the milestone.[^cite_note-498]

### 2018: World Cup disappointment



A mural of Neymar ahead of the [2018 World Cup](13327177.md) in [Kazan](202295.md), Russia

&#x20;Neymar was rushed back prior to the [2018 World Cup](13327177.md) in Russia following his injury in February with PSG.[^cite_note-\:8-179] In May 2018, he was named in [Tite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tite_\(football_manager\))'s final 23-man [squad](149286.md) for the 2018 World Cup in Russia.[^cite_note-499] On 3 June 2018, he made his international comeback after a three-month absence from a foot injury and scored a goal in a 2–0 win over [Croatia](5573.md) in a friendly match at [Anfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anfield).[^cite_note-500] His playing style was not quite the same as he appeared to be avoiding physical contact entirely rather than simply trying to draw fouls.[^cite_note-\:8-179] The following week, he became Brazil's joint-third highest goalscorer alongside Romário when he scored his 55th international goal in a 3–0 friendly win over [Austria](695226.md).[^cite_note-501]&#x20;



Neymar during the national anthem of Brazil at the 2018 World Cup

In Brazil's opening match at the World Cup against [Switzerland](681862.md), Neymar was noted as their main threat, which was proved by him being fouled 10 times during the match.[^cite_note-\:74-502] This was the most fouls a player had suffered in a World Cup match since [Alan Shearer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Shearer) against [Tunisia](1029953.md) in [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup), who was fouled 11 times.[^cite_note-503] Switzerland were able to limit Neymar throughout the match, with it ending a 1–1 draw.[^cite_note-\:74-502] In Brazil's second World Cup match, played against [Costa Rica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa_Rica_national_football_team) at the [Krestovsky Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krestovsky_Stadium), Saint Petersburg, on 22 June, Neymar scored the team's second goal in injury time as Brazil won 2–0.[^cite_note-504] The goal, his 56th for Brazil, took him third in the all-time scoring chart behind only Pelé and [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\)).[^cite_note-505] On 2 July, Neymar scored his second goal of the World Cup in a 2–0 win over Mexico in the last 16, while also assisting [Roberto Firmino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Firmino) for his goal.[^cite_note-506]

After the Mexico game, [BBC Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sport) wrote that despite Neymar's "good performances" coming up top in several statistics at the World Cup, "there is still a sense that he remains widely unpopular among neutrals" due to his "petulance and the play-acting". Meanwhile, Brazilian newspaper *[O Globo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Globo)* wrote that "Neymar has charmed Brazil, but annoyed the whole world".[^cite_note-507] On 6 July, Brazil were eliminated after losing 2–1 against [Belgium](679716.md) in the quarter-finals, with Neymar denied an injury time equaliser by a finger-tip save from Belgium goalkeeper [Thibaut Courtois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thibaut_Courtois).[^cite_note-508]

### 2019–2021: Copa América runner-up

In May 2019, Neymar was included in Brazil's 23-man squad for the 2019 Copa América on home soil.[^cite_note-509] On 5 June, however, he suffered an ankle injury in a 2–0 friendly win over [Qatar](1111350.md) and was ruled out of the tournament, with Neymar expected to miss four weeks due to the injury.[^cite_note-510][^cite_note-511]

On 10 October 2019, Neymar played his 100th match for Brazil in a friendly draw 1–1 with [Senegal](1017436.md) in Singapore.[^cite_note-\:50-512] On 13 October 2020, he scored a [hat-trick](411541.md#Association_football) in a [2022 World Cup qualifier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(CONMEBOL\)) against [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peru_national_football_team), which Brazil won 4–2. He reached his 64th international goal and surpassed Ronaldo as the nation's second highest goalscorer of all time.[^cite_note-513]

Towards the end of 2020, Neymar was inducted into the [Maracanã](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maracan%C3%A3_Stadium) Hall of Fame.[^cite_note-\:27-265] In early 2021, the IFFHS ranked him in second place behind Messi for their [CONMEBOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONMEBOL) [The Best Man Player of the Decade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFFHS_World's_Best_Player#The_Best_Man_Player_of_the_Decade_\(2011%E2%80%932020\)) (2011–2020) award.[^cite_note-\:44-315] On 13 June 2021, in Brazil's opening group match of the [2021 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica), which took place on home soil, Neymar scored his team's second goal from the penalty spot and later assisted another for [Gabriel Barbosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Barbosa) in a 3–0 win over Venezuela.[^cite_note-514] In the following match on 17 June, he once again scored the second goal in an eventual 4–0 win over Peru.[^cite_note-515] On 23 June he assisted [Casemiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casemiro)'s match–winning goal from a corner in injury time of a 2–1 win over Colombia.[^cite_note-516] On 2 July, he assisted [Lucas Paquetá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Paquet%C3%A1)'s goal in a 1–0 win over Chile in the quarter-finals of the competition.[^cite_note-517] Three days later, he once again assisted Paquetá for the only goal of the match in the semi-finals against Peru.[^cite_note-518] On 10 July, Brazil were defeated 1–0 by [Argentina](454699.md) in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica_Final).[^cite_note-Copa_América_final-519] Despite the loss, Neymar received the [Golden Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica#Awards) alongside Argentina's Messi for his performances throughout the competition.[^cite_note-520][^cite_note-\:54-521]

On 9 September 2021, Neymar scored the second goal in a 2–0 win over Peru and became Brazil's all-time top scorer in [World Cup qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_qualification) matches, with 12 in total.[^cite_note-\:47-522]

### 2022–2023: Third World Cup and Brazil's all-time top scorer



Neymar playing against [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_national_football_team) in the group stage of the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md)

On 7 November 2022, Neymar was named in the Brazil squad for the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md).[^cite_note-523] Heading into the World Cup, Neymar was arguably in the best form of his career during the 2022–23 season, scoring 15 goals and providing 11 assists in just 20 games.[^cite_note-\:11-343][^cite_note-\:12-524][^cite_note-525][^cite_note-SkyWC2022-347] According to Maram Albaharna and several other sports journalists, he was a large part of the reason why Brazil were favourites to win the World Cup.[^cite_note-526][^cite_note-\:11-343] The 2022 World Cup was touted to potentially be Neymar's crowning moment for his career.[^cite_note-527] Neymar, alongside Messi and Mbappé, was widely favourite to win the Golden Ball.[^cite_note-528][^cite_note-529]

&#x20;Neymar played 79 minutes of Brazil's first match on 24 November and assisted the opening goal[^cite_note-530] in a 2–0 win against Serbia, but was unable to play the next two group games due to ankle ligament damage.[^cite_note-531] Neymar recovered from his injury to play against [South Korea](1018627.md) in the last 16 on 5 December, where he assisted Vinícius Júnior's opening goal and scored a penalty in a 4–1 win for Brazil;[^cite_note-532] as a result, he became only the third Brazilian player ever, after Pelé and Ronaldo, to score in three different World Cups.[^cite_note-Neymar_ties_Pele-533]

> 'Keep inspiring us. I will keep punching the air with joy for every goal you score'

— [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9) congratulates Neymar on equalling his record of 77 official international goals.

[^cite_note-534]

Four days later, in the quarter-final match against [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team), Neymar lit the match up in extra time with a "magical" run before scoring; he played two one-twos with his teammates before quickly finding himself in the box and going past the goalkeeper to score into the empty net.[^cite_note-535] Journalist [Michael Cox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Cox_\(journalist\)) described it as one of the "greatest individual goals in football history".[^cite_note-\:31-536] It was Neymar's 77th international goal, which equalled Pelé's official goal tally for the national team.[^cite_note-\:31-536] Brazil, however, suffered a shock defeat, losing 4–2 in the ensuing penalty shootout following a 1–1 draw after extra time, before Neymar was able to take Brazil's final penalty.[^cite_note-Neymar_ties_Pele-533][^cite_note-537] Neymar was distraught on the pitch; overwhelmed with emotion, he hinted that it could be his last game for Brazil.[^cite_note-538] In a later social media post, he said that the defeat to Croatia "psychologically destroyed" him and that it was the defeat that had affected him the most in his career, causing him to be "paralysed" for 10 minutes after the match.[^cite_note-539]

On 8 September 2023, Neymar scored twice for Brazil in a 5–1 win over [Bolivia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivia_national_football_team) in their opening match for [2026 FIFA World Cup qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(CONMEBOL\)). His 78th and 79th goals in his 125th appearance for Brazil, Neymar became the [national team's top goalscorer](149286.md#Top_goalscorers) ahead of Pelé.[^cite_note-540][^cite_note-TNT-541] Mimicking Pelé's punch in the air goal celebration with the first of his two goals, Neymar stated after the game that he never imagined he would reach the record.[^cite_note-TNT-541][^cite_note-542] Brazil coach [Fernando Diniz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Diniz) declared that Neymar is one of the greatest players in history following this achievement,[^cite_note-543] whilst *[The Athletic](54936152.md)* asserted that Neymar should be remembered as "one of the best the game has seen".[^cite_note-\:31-536] His 79 goals for Brazil also makes him the [second-highest South American men's goalscorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_men's_footballers_with_50_or_more_international_goals). A month later, he tied former American player [Landon Donovan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landon_Donovan) on achieving a record 58 assists (in 127 matches) in international football; in the 50th minute of Brazil's World Cup qualifier against Venezuela, his corner kick was headed in by defender [Gabriel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Magalh%C3%A3es).[^cite_note-\:20-544] Nearly exactly two years later, the record was surpassed by Messi in October 2025, who reached 60 assists, though it took him considerably more matches to do so (195).[^cite_note-545]

### 2023–2026: Long-term injury hiatus, final World Cup and retirement



Neymar ahead of the [2026 World Cup](36581929.md) group stage match against [Morocco](1024865.md) on 14 June

On 17 October 2023, in Brazil's 2–0 qualifying defeat to [Uruguay](679782.md), Neymar was taken off in tears on a stretcher at the end of the first half, following an awkward landing after a challenge. Neymar would confirm later that week that he had ruptured his [ACL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anterior_cruciate_ligament) and [meniscus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meniscus_\(anatomy\)) completely in his left knee and would need to undergo surgery, likely ruling him out for the remainder of the 2023–24 campaign;[^cite_note-ACL_injury-377] consequently, he was left out of Brazil's squad for the [2024 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica).[^cite_note-546] On 2 March 2025, he was selected in the 29-men in the preliminary squad for the national team.[^cite_note-547] However, he would make no appearances with the national team throughout the year.[^cite_note-548]

On 18 May 2026, Neymar was named to the 26-man squad for the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md).[^cite_note-549] Later that month, he sustained a calf injury that ruled him out for two to three weeks, causing him to miss the friendly matches leading up to the tournament.[^cite_note-550] On 24 June, he made his first appearance at the 2026 World Cup in Brazil's final group match, coming on as a substitute in a 3–0 victory over [Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_national_football_team). It was his first appearance with Brazil in over two years.[^cite_note-551] Hence, he became the fourth Brazilian player to feature in four World Cup tournaments, following Pelé, [Djalma Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djalma_Santos) and [Cafu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cafu).[^cite_note-552] On 5 July, Neymar scored from a penalty in a 2–1 loss to [Norway](203277.md) in the round of 16; it was his ninth World Cup goal, making him only the second Brazilian player to score in four World Cups after Pelé.[^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554]

Following the defeat, Neymar announced his retirement from international football, ending his career at [MetLife Stadium](5476983.md), the same stadium where he made his debut for Brazil, with 80 goals in 130 appearances.[^cite_note-555] Later that month, Neymar confirmed his retirement from international football, stating that his time with the national team had "passed".[^cite_note-556]

## Player profile

### Style of play



Neymar controlling the ball in an exhibition match in 2011

Primarily playing as an [attacking midfielder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacking_midfielder) or [winger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winger_\(association_football\)), Neymar is prolific in scoring and has high-level [playmaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playmaker) abilities.[^cite_note-557] He has scored over 450 goals and has registered over 250 [assists](1167721.md).[^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559][^cite_note-560] For much of his early career, including at Santos and Barcelona, he was usually deployed on the left side of a front three for both club and country in a 4–3–3 formation due to his pace and playmaking skills;[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-\:40-562][^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] this position allowed him to shoot with his stronger foot and create chances for teammates. His main traits are his vision, agility, passing, finishing, [dribbling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dribbling#Association_football), [feints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dummy_\(football\)#Association_football), and first touch, being described as both "electric" and "explosive".[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566][^cite_note-567] He is also known for often using the [rainbow flick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainbow_flick).[^cite_note-568] Neymar is two-footed and can score [free-kicks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_free_kick) and [penalties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penalty_kick_\(association_football\)).[^cite_note-tuttomercatoweb.com2-569][^cite_note-generazioneditalenti.it-570]

Considered to be a highly promising player in his youth, Neymar was described early in his career by the media as "a true phenomenon".[^cite_note-571][^cite_note-\:22-572][^cite_note-tuttomercatoweb.com2-569] At Santos, Neymar was renowned for his explosive pace and flair, often beating defenders with ease and showcasing excessive skill moves — frequently bordering on showboating.[^cite_note-\:40-562] At Barcelona, he became more efficient in providing and more refined, improving his passing and overall decision-making.[^cite_note-573] After joining PSG, he evolved into a complete number 10, capable of dictating play and executing highly difficult passes.[^cite_note-574] Reflecting on his years at PSG, Neymar said that he experienced his prime years at the club; in 2021, a journalist said that PSG "relies almost entirely on his creative genius".[^cite_note-\:30-575][^cite_note-576][^cite_note-577]

According to a writer for *Goal*, Neymar "is a master dribbler, and he sees openings that other players don't. He can create chances out of nothing and more often than not, has the finishing touch or decisive final pass to match his ingenuity on the ball."[^cite_note-\:8-179] A sports writer for *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* praised him as "an increasingly rare phenomenon in a modern game of academy-refined talent",[^cite_note-578] whilst another of *The Independent* opined that Messi was the only player superior in "every facet of the game" to him during his 10-year tenure in Europe.[^cite_note-579]

Neymar's playing style has often made him a target for rough treatment from defenders.[^cite_note-\:59-580] A report in early 2022 revealed that he was the most fouled player in Europe since 2016.[^cite_note-581] During his time in Ligue 1, he was fouled more than any other player, which caused frequent injuries.[^cite_note-\:59-580]

### Reception

Neymar has drawn high praise from current and former players, with many either calling him one of the greatest players of all time, the best player they have played with, or the best player they have ever seen.[^cite_note-589] [David Beckham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Beckham) praised him as "an outstanding talent, a once-in-a-generation type of footballer who has fans on their feet whenever he gets the ball."[^cite_note-590] In the view of [Thierry Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thierry_Henry), Neymar changed football with the way he plays.[^cite_note-591] [Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deco) said that Neymar "plays beautiful football" and is "one of the greatest".[^cite_note-GoalDeco-587] [Rio Ferdinand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Ferdinand) said that Neymar "could do things that no other player in the world could do."[^cite_note-592] When asked who his top five favourite dribblers are, Benzema responded "Neymar, and that's all."[^cite_note-593] In 2015, [Pierre-Emerick Aubameyang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Emerick_Aubameyang) was asked in an interview which one player he would like his club Dortmund to sign, to which he answered "Neymar".[^cite_note-UEFAErrors-101]

&#x20;In 2022, sports writer [Jonathan Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Wilson_\(writer\)) summarised Neymar:

> "As a great who has been burdened by his talent \[...] someone whose transfers torpedoed Barcelona and initiated the boom-and-bust cycle of the past four years, it is Neymar, more than anybody else who embodies the age."[^cite_note-\:13-594]

&#x20;Many players have cited Neymar as the toughest opponent they have faced in their career.[^cite_note-595] Despite still being in his early 30s, players of the new era of football have singled out Neymar's influence, with many calling him their idol.[^cite_note-596][^cite_note-PlanetFootball2024-584] After being congratulated by [Marcelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_\(footballer,_born_1988\)) following PSG's Champions League final win in [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_UEFA_Champions_League_final), [Désiré Doué](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%A9sir%C3%A9_Dou%C3%A9) said to him "say hello to Neymar, he's my idol”.[^cite_note-597] Similarly, following Spain's triumph at the 2026 World Cup, [Nico Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_Williams) dedicated the victory to his "idol" Neymar.[^cite_note-598]





Despite the dominance of the [Messi–Ronaldo rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messi%E2%80%93Ronaldo_rivalry), some regarded Neymar as one of the two best players in the world with Messi.

Neymar is "universally deemed football's best player after Ronaldo and Messi" according to *Sports Illustrated*,[^cite_note-599] however, many have regarded him as second only to Messi — or even his equal.[^cite_note-601] Opta Sports performance statistics showed that Neymar finished above second place Messi as the best-performing player in Europe in 2016–17 and 2017–18.[^cite_note-\:42-171][^cite_note-WhoScoredPSG-232] He has also been hailed as being on the same level as Messi and Ronaldo[^cite_note-602][^cite_note-GoalDeco-587] and was considered the best player in the world at various points in his career.[^cite_note-\:555-603][^cite_note-iNews2020-282][^cite_note-\:17-281][^cite_note-\:18-280][^cite_note-\:30-575][^cite_note-\:15-133] *Goal* claimed that at Neymar's best form, there are a few players better than him, crediting him as among the most talented players ever.[^cite_note-604]

The term *Neymardependencia* (Neymar dependency) is often used in Brazil to refer to the extent to which the national team relies on him to guide their success.[^cite_note-605][^cite_note-SkyWC2022-347][^cite_note-606] In 2014, the [Associated Press](18935732.md) said "despite being only 22 years old, Neymar has already done enough to be ranked among Brazil’s greatest players of all time."[^cite_note-607] According to a writer for *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, Neymar was "carrying a relatively weak Brazil side for much of his career under immense pressure."[^cite_note-608] [1970 FIFA World Cup](157227.md) champion [Tostão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tost%C3%A3o) opinionated that Neymar is better than Ronaldo, Romário, [Rivaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivaldo), and [Kaká](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kak%C3%A1) because he "does everything during a game".[^cite_note-609] In the view of *[GiveMeSport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GiveMeSport)*, he might be considered the best Brazilian player of all time if he shone in an era without Messi and Ronaldo.[^cite_note-\:132-610] In Brazil, he is often referred to as *O Príncipe* ('The Prince'), a title that reflects a symbolic parallel to Pelé, who is widely known as *O Rei* ('The King').[^cite_note-611][^cite_note-612][^cite_note-613]

Neymar has also been praised by managers, including [José Mourinho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mourinho), who claimed that the unprecedented €222 million transfer fee to sign Neymar in 2017 was not expensive for a player of his caliber.[^cite_note-BBCTransfer2017-183] Liverpool manager [Jürgen Klopp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%BCrgen_Klopp) drew attention to the threat of Neymar ahead of PSG's first match against Liverpool of the Champions League group stage in September 2018: "We'll try to avoid passes to him. We'll try to avoid him winning challenges \[...] He's a fantastic footballer, an outstanding player."[^cite_note-614] Ahead of the Champions League semi-final in 2021, Man City's manager [Pep Guardiola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pep_Guardiola) called him "a joy to watch".[^cite_note-615]

### Criticisms

> "And so, circus Neymar arrived in Dortmund. The Brazilian's moods, histrionics, petulance, and goalscoring prowess were on view in every fascinating and exasperating detail."

— *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* on some of the traits associated with Neymar following a Champions League game for PSG against Borussia Dortmund, February 2020.

[^cite_note-616]

Neymar has been known and criticised for his excessive [diving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diving_\(association_football\))[^cite_note-617] when tackled by another player. Brazilian legend Pelé has said, "\[h]e is a player with a body that can't take a lot of hits. \[...] A lot of times he will fall because he can't do anything else, but he was overdoing it." He continued: "Even when he is fouled, he can't make a spectacle out of it".[^cite_note-618] During the 2018 World Cup, Neymar inspired the "Neymar Challenge" on social media due to his dramatic dives.[^cite_note-619][^cite_note-620] As the star player for Brazil and PSG, he was often the most targeted player on the field; in 2018, he was the most fouled player in the top five leagues.[^cite_note-621] A divisive figure in the sport, he has been accused of exaggerating injury, with [Eric Cantona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Cantona) stating Neymar is a "great actor", comparing him to a wheeled suitcase: "you barely touch it, and it turns round and round for hours."[^cite_note-622][^cite_note-623] In response to the criticism over his petulance and histrionics, Neymar starred in a commercial after the 2018 World Cup where he said that he wasn't able to control his frustrations, which contributed to his theatrics, and he promised to change for the better.[^cite_note-624]

Neymar has also been criticised for his lack of professional discipline, with his desire to party and play late-night poker games. In 2018, Pelé criticised Neymar: "It's difficult to defend Neymar for all the things he does besides playing football. I've been with him in Europe twice. We talked and I explained: 'The God of football gave you the gift. What you do complicates it'."[^cite_note-\:8-179] In February 2023, the day after PSG lost to Bayern Munich in the first leg of the Champions League round of 16, images of Neymar in a fast-food restaurant surfaced on social media. Neymar defended himself, saying "It's my life, I do what I want."[^cite_note-Le_Monde.fr2-363]



Neymar's failure to win the [Ballon d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballon_d'Or) is often discussed as being a disappointment in his career.

[^cite_note-FourFourTwo2025-362]

Early in his career, particularly after his stellar 2014–15 season with Barcelona at 23 years old, Neymar was unanimously expected to win the Ballon d'Or at some point in his career.[^cite_note-625][^cite_note-626] He has been nominated for the Ballon d'Or nine times, which is more than any other Brazilian player in history (except Ronaldo), and has finished in the top 10 six times — only Thierry Henry and Zidane (seven), Mbappé (eight), Messi (14), and Ronaldo (15) have more.[^cite_note-\:27-265] In 2023, following his departure from Europe to Saudi Arabia at a young age (31) without winning the Ballon d'Or, various media outlets dubbed Neymar as the 'Prince who never became King'.[^cite_note-\:62-372][^cite_note-\:72-373][^cite_note-BBCPrince2025-213] *Sports Illustrated* claimed that Neymar could end his career as the best player never to win a Ballon d'Or, joining the likes of Henry and Iniesta.[^cite_note-627] Others have also expressed surprise that Neymar has never won the Ballon d'Or, including former goalkeeper [Gianluigi Buffon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianluigi_Buffon), who opinionated that he should have won at least five Ballon d'Ors.[^cite_note-\:642-583] [Thomas Meunier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Meunier), who played with him at PSG, said that it is a "pity" that that he did not win a Ballon d'Or.[^cite_note-\:02-585] Former Manchester United defender [Axel Tuanzebe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axel_Tuanzebe), who faced Neymar, claimed that he should have won all the Ballon d’Ors from 2017 onwards.[^cite_note-\:232-628]

In a 2025 interview, Neymar responded to the claims that he did not reach the heights expected of him: "Many things happened, I had many injuries that cost me a lot in my career".[^cite_note-\:192-629] In spite of this, he expressed, "I achieved almost everything I dreamed of, and I even achieved things I never dreamed of."[^cite_note-\:192-629] Wilson of *Sports Illustrated* gives the view that, "The early hype \[around Neymar] was absurd, and if he has not lived up to it, it is largely because nobody could have."[^cite_note-\:13-594]

## Personal life

### Religion



Neymar expresses his Christian faith after winning the gold medal with [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil_national_under-23_football_team) at the [2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Summer_Olympics).

Neymar is a [Christian](5211.md) and he was baptised at Peniel Baptist Church in [Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santos,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) in 2008.[^cite_note-630] He speaks regularly about his faith.[^cite_note-631] Neymar has said about his faith: "Life only makes sense when our highest ideal is to serve [Christ](1095706.md)!"[^cite_note-632][^cite_note-neymarjr-5][^cite_note-instagram.com-6][^cite_note-633] Additionally, he has worn a headband with the words "100% Jesus".[^cite_note-634] Neymar also [tithes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tithe) some of his income to his church and has named [Kaká](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kak%C3%A1) as his religious role model.[^cite_note-635]

### Relationships and children

From 2013 to 2017, Neymar was in a highly publicized relationship with Brazilian actress [Bruna Marquezine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruna_Marquezine).[^cite_note-636][^cite_note-637][^cite_note-638] Neymar has fathered 4 children with 3 different women, including 1 son and 3 daughters.[^cite_note-639][^cite_note-640] Since 2022, he's been in an on and off relationship with influencer Bruna Biancardi, with whom he's fathered 2 daughters with.[^cite_note-\:23-641]

### Charity

Every year, Neymar organises a charity match with fellow Brazilian footballer [Nenê](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nen%C3%AA_\(footballer,_born_1981\)) in Nenê's hometown of [Jundiaí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jundia%C3%AD), with the purpose of raising food for poor families.[^cite_note-642] In addition to his native [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language), Neymar also speaks Spanish.[^cite_note-643][^cite_note-644]

### Legal

In July 2019, police announced that a rape investigation against Neymar would be dropped due to insufficient evidence.[^cite_note-645] On 2 September 2020, it was reported that Neymar, along with PSG teammates [Ángel Di María](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81ngel_Di_Mar%C3%ADa) and [Leandro Paredes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leandro_Paredes), tested positive for COVID-19. The French sports newspaper *[L'Équipe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'%C3%89quipe)* said that the three players reportedly went on vacation in [Ibiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibiza). As a result, they were quarantined for one week, and the rest of the players and working staff were scheduled to take a coronavirus test within the same week.[^cite_note-646][^cite_note-647]

During a match between PSG and Marseille, Neymar reported being racially abused by [Álvaro González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_Gonz%C3%A1lez_\(footballer,_born_1990\)), after being sent off for hitting González in the back of the head.[^cite_note-648] The Spanish defender denied making any racist comments towards Neymar.[^cite_note-649] On 16 September, Neymar was handed a two-match ban for his actions; Ligue 1 also began an investigation into the alleged racist comments made by Álvaro.[^cite_note-650] Several days before the decision made by the French league, Spanish radio station [Cadena SER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadena_SER) claimed to have footage of Neymar racially abusing Marseille player [Hiroki Sakai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroki_Sakai).[^cite_note-651] On 30 September, Ligue 1 decided that both Álvaro and Neymar would receive no suspensions as there was insufficient evidence of their wrongdoings.[^cite_note-652] Additionally, Sakai denied any racist comments from Neymar on social media.[^cite_note-653]

In the summer of 2023, [Agence France-Presse](471981.md), citing local authorities, reported that Neymar faced a potential fine of about \$1 million for environmental violations committed during the construction of his [Mangaratiba](8856127.md) mansion. The process of building his mansion diverted the waterway and used beach sand without permission.[^cite_note-654]

Santos opened an investigation into Neymar after the player allegedly physically assaulted [Robinho Júnior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robinho_J%C3%BAnior) by tripping him and giving him a "violent slap to the face" in retaliation for Robinho Júnior dribbling past him during a training session at the [CT Rei Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CT_Rei_Pel%C3%A9) on 3 May 2026.[^cite_note-655][^cite_note-656] Neymar later apologised for the incident,[^cite_note-657] and Robinho Júnior's representatives subsequently gave up on notifying the club.[^cite_note-658]

### Politics



Neymar and Brazilian President [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md). He supported Bolsonaro in the [2022 Brazilian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election).

[^cite_note-\:3-659]

Neymar supported Brazilian President [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md) in the [2022 Brazilian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election).[^cite_note-\:3-659]

## Outside football

### Wealth and sponsorships



Neymar's 2014 World Cup jersey on display at an exhibition in the [Franz Mayer Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Mayer_Museum), [Mexico City](18987.md).

Neymar has signed many sponsorships since his reputation began to grow from the age of 17. In March 2011, he signed an 11-year contract with American sportswear company Nike,[^cite_note-660] which ended in August 2020 after a Nike employee made a sexual assault complaint against him.[^cite_note-661][^cite_note-662] Since then, Neymar has signed with [Puma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puma_\(brand\)).[^cite_note-663] He adopted the famous PUMA King boots, previously worn by the likes of Pelé, Maradona, [Johan Cruyff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johan_Cruyff), and [Eusébio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eus%C3%A9bio).[^cite_note-\:52-664] Neymar's deal with Puma was considered the highest-value individual football boot contract ever, with him earning around €25 million per year, surpassing deals held by the likes of Ronaldo and Messi.[^cite_note-665] In March 2011, [Panasonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panasonic) paid US\$2.4 million to secure Neymar's services for two years. In 2012, *France Football* had ranked Neymar 13th in its list of the world's richest players, with total earnings of \$18.8 million for the previous 12 months.[^cite_note-666] He has also signed other sponsorships from [Volkswagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen), Tenys Pé Baruel, Lupo, [Ambev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambev), [Claro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claro_Americas), [Unilever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unilever), and [Santander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santander_Group).[^cite_note-667][^cite_note-668][^cite_note-669][^cite_note-670]

In 2019, *Forbes* ranked Neymar the [world's third-highest-paid athlete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes'_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_athletes) (after Messi and Ronaldo) with earnings of \$105 million for the year;[^cite_note-Forbes-671] he was also ranked fourth in 2020 (\$95.5 million) and 2021 (\$95 million), seventh in 2024 (\$108 million), twelfth in 2023 (\$85 million) and 25th in 2025 (\$76 million).[^cite_note-672][^cite_note-673][^cite_note-674][^cite_note-675][^cite_note-676] [*Sportico*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penske_Media_Corporation#Sportico) named him the fourth highest-paid athlete of 2022 and put him at sixth place in 2021, 2023 and 2024.[^cite_note-677][^cite_note-678][^cite_note-679][^cite_note-680] In 2018, *France Football* ranked Neymar the third-highest-paid footballer in the world, earning a total income for €81.5m (\$95m) for the year.[^cite_note-\:1-681]

On 8 May 2013, Neymar was named by *SportsPro* magazine as the most marketable athlete in the world, ahead of Messi (2nd) and Ronaldo (8th).[^cite_note-SportsPro-682] The same month, Neymar launched the Nike Hypervenom football boot.[^cite_note-683] In November 2012, advertising agency Loducca created Neymar's own personal brand logo,[^cite_note-brand-684] featuring the N, J, and R (Neymar Junior) with the N styled to match Neymar's shirt number 11.[^cite_note-brand-684] Neymar starred in a 2014 advert for [Beats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beats_Electronics) alongside other football stars such as Henry and Suárez, with the theme of 'The Game Before The Game' and the players pre-game ritual of listening to music.[^cite_note-685] In 2018, Neymar was highly influential to PSG’s groundbreaking partnership with [Air Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Jordan). In June 2016, he became the first footballer ever to wear Air Jordan-branded boots—a custom Air Jordan V Hypervenom—establishing an early and personal connection with Air Jordan.[^cite_note-686] That relationship set the stage for the 2018 PSG × Air Jordan launch.[^cite_note-\:48-687] Neymar himself described Air Jordan as "phenomenal, magic, irreverent," calling it a perfect fit for both Paris and the Champions League.[^cite_note-\:48-687][^cite_note-688]

In the later stages of his career, Neymar has been regularly seen playing high limit [poker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poker), including live streamed celebrity games from California's [Hustler Casino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hustler_Casino).[^cite_note-689][^cite_note-690] In 2025, boxer [Conor Benn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conor_Benn) called out his rival boxer [Chris Eubank Jr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Eubank_Jr). at a pre-fight press conference, alleging Eubank Jr. owed Neymar £4 million in poker debts.[^cite_note-691] Around May 2025, a 31 year old anonymous Brazilian billionaire from [Porto Alegre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porto_Alegre) left Neymar over \$1 billion in his will, saying he had not wanted to give his money to the [government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Brazil) or his estranged family.[^cite_note-692] The billionaire had claimed Neymar’s relationship with his father reminded him of his own, who is deceased. He also had stated that he saw Neymar to “not be greedy”. The will was formalized on June 12, 2025.

### Media



Neymar at the [Red Bull Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Arena_\(New_Jersey\)) in [Harrison, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison,_New_Jersey) during a match between [New York Red Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Red_Bulls) and [LA Galaxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LA_Galaxy) in 2011

Neymar was featured on the front covers of the video games *[Pro Evolution Soccer 2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Evolution_Soccer_2012)* and *[Pro Evolution Soccer 2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Evolution_Soccer_2013)* on the North American version, after [Konami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konami) Digital Entertainment announced that he had joined Pro Evolution Soccer.[^cite_note-693] Neymar joined Ronaldo as a featured cover athlete.[^cite_note-694][^cite_note-695] Neymar also features in [EA Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EA_Sports)' [*FIFA* video game series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_\(video_game_series\)), with the trailer for *[FIFA 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_18)* showing him in his PSG home jersey.[^cite_note-696] He appeared alongside Ronaldo on the Champions and Ultimate Edition packs for *[FIFA 19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_19)*, where Neymar's "Hang Loose" goal celebration also features in the game.[^cite_note-697][^cite_note-698] Neymar also appears as a playable operator in the 2022 [first-person shooter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First-person_shooter) *[Call of Duty: Modern Warfare II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Call_of_Duty:_Modern_Warfare_II_\(2022_video_game\))* alongside fellow football players [Paul Pogba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Pogba) and Messi,[^cite_note-PogbaNeymarMessi-699] whose character models were released as [downloadable content](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downloadable_content) to coincide with the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md).[^cite_note-mw2neymar-700]

Neymar appeared on the cover of *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TIME_\(magazine\))* magazine in February 2013, the first Brazilian athlete to do so. The issue included an article by Ghosh entitled "The Next Pelé" and subtitled "How the career of Brazilian football star Neymar explains his country's economy".[^cite_note-701] In 2017, he was the only footballer included in the *Time* [100 Most Influential People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) list.[^cite_note-702]

Controversy erupted because of a cover by Brazilian football magazine *[Placar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Placar)* that depicted Neymar on a cross. The title read "A Crucificação de Neymar" (the Crucifixion of Neymar) and subtitled: "the Brazilian ace turns scapegoat in a sport where everyone plays dirty".[^cite_note-703]

In April 2013, Brazilian cartoonist [Mauricio de Sousa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauricio_de_Sousa) released a *[Monica's Gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica's_Gang)* comic book featuring a younger version of Neymar (called *Neymar Jr.*) as the main character.[^cite_note-704]

In May 2013, *SportsPro* magazine named Neymar as the most marketable athlete on the planet for the second consecutive year. He topped the list ahead of Lionel Messi, [Rory McIlroy](3949844.md), [Usain Bolt](4764846.md) and Ronaldo, among other sportspeople.[^cite_note-SportsPro-682] The list measures the monetary value, the age, the force in domestic markets, the charisma and their market potential in the next three years.[^cite_note-SportsPro-682] In March 2015, Neymar had the fourth highest social media rank in the world among sportspeople, behind Ronaldo, Messi and Beckham, with 52 million Facebook fans.[^cite_note-705][^cite_note-706] He has [over 200 million Instagram followers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most-followed_Instagram_accounts), the third highest for a sportsperson (after Ronaldo and Messi), and inside the top 20 most followed people.[^cite_note-707] In ESPN's list of active sportspeople in 2016, Neymar was ranked the fourth most famous athlete in the world.[^cite_note-ESPN_100-708] In April 2017, Neymar was included in the [Time 100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100), *Time* magazine's list of the most influential people in the world.[^cite_note-\:0-709] In 2019, ESPN again ranked him the world's fourth most famous athlete.[^cite_note-710]

To mark the [World Cup](656933.md) commencing in Brazil, in June 2014, Neymar appeared with supermodel [Gisele Bündchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisele_B%C3%BCndchen) on the front cover of [*Vogue*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(magazine\))'s Brazilian edition.[^cite_note-711] In November 2014, Neymar appeared in FIFA's "11 against [Ebola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebola_virus_disease)" campaign with a selection of top football players from around the world, including Ronaldo, [Gareth Bale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gareth_Bale), Xavi and [Didier Drogba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didier_Drogba).[^cite_note-Ebola-712] Under the slogan "Together, we can beat Ebola", FIFA's campaign was done in conjunction with the [Confederation of African Football](715265.md) and health experts, with the players holding up eleven messages to raise awareness of the disease and ways to combat it.[^cite_note-Ebola-712][^cite_note-713]

In collaboration with [DAZN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DAZN), in 2019 Neymar appeared in *The Making Of* series, documentaries which relives the significant games that helped define football's greatest modern icons, with Neymar recounting his performance for Santos against Flamengo in 2011 when he scored that year's FIFA Puskás Award for Goal of the Year,[^cite_note-714] and also the film *Neymar and the Line of King* which covers Neymar's journey in becoming one of Brazil's top players.[^cite_note-715][^cite_note-716]

In December 2021, he signed a deal with Facebook Gaming.[^cite_note-717] He was also the subject of a 2022 [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix) [documentary series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Documentary_film) titled *[Neymar: The Perfect Chaos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neymar:_The_Perfect_Chaos)*.[^cite_note-718]

In June 2024, Neymar Jr. was appointed brand ambassador for Blaze's exclusive "crash" online gaming product, promoting the sports betting platform's new multiplier‐based wager.[^cite_note-719]

### Political views

In the days leading up to the [2022 Brazilian presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_presidential_election), Neymar appeared in a [TikTok](56822861.md) video where he lip-synced a campaign jingle promoting the country's [right-wing](56522.md) and [socially conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_conservatism) [President](273638.md) [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md).[^cite_note-720][^cite_note-721] He received heavy criticism for his endorsement.[^cite_note-722] Bolsonaro was defeated in the election by [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md).

### Music

Neymar has become an icon in promoting Brazilian modern pop music, particularly [Música sertaneja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BAsica_sertaneja). The video in which Neymar dances in the [Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santos_FC) [locker room](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Locker_room) in front of teammates carrying his recorder making the round of the players and making them react to the tune of [Michel Teló](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Tel%C3%B3)'s hit "[Ai se eu te pego!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ai_se_eu_te_pego!)" went [viral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viral_video). He made a point of performing his dance antics to the song after scoring goals in football games and appeared live with Teló in one of the latter's concerts. He also supported sertanejo singer [Gusttavo Lima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gusttavo_Lima) performing live with Lima on renditions of the singer's hits "[Balada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balada_\(song\))" and "Fazer Beber". In 2012, he made cameo appearances in the [music video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_video) for yet another sertanejo hit "[Eu Quero Tchu, Eu Quero Tcha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eu_Quero_Tchu,_Eu_Quero_Tcha)" by [João Lucas & Marcelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Lucas_&_Marcelo). In 2013, Neymar appeared on a rap music video, "País do Futebol" by [MC Guimê](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MC_Guim%C3%AA).[^cite_note-723]
